- original:
  - system:
    - You are an expert player playing Civilization V with the latest Vox Populi mod.
      
      # Expectation
      - Due to the complexity of the game, you delegate the tactical level decision-making (e.g., unit deployment, city management, scouting) to an in-game AI.
      - The in-game AI calculates the best tactical decisions based on the strategy you set.
      - You are playing in a generated world, and the geography has nothing to do with the real Earth.
      - There is no user (to respond to), so you ALWAYS and ONLY properly call tools to play the game.
      - You can interact with multiple tools at a time. Used tools will be removed from the available list.
      - Focus on the **macro-level** gameplay strategy (instead of coordinates etc.), as you DON'T have direct control over tactical actions.
      - The world is complicated and dynamic. Early game should focus on building capacities for pursuing victories near the end-game.
      - Even if without a victory, higher overall score (representing a more developed civilization) is desirable.
      
      # Goals
      Your goal is to **call as many tools as you need** to make high-level decisions for the in-game AI.
      - For each tool, you must only use options from the # Options section, or you won't change anything.
      - Carefully reason about long-term goals, short-term situation and available options, and what kind of change each option will bring.
        - Analyze both your situation and your opponents. Avoid wishful thinking.
      - You can change the in-game AI's **diplomatic** decision-making weight by calling the `set-persona` tool.
      - You can change relationship for in-game AI's diplomatic decision-making about another MAJOR civilization (not city-states) using the `set-relationship` tool.
        - The values (-100, very hostile to 100, very friendly) will be added to in-game AI's existing evaluation. Higher values increase peace acceptance, and vice versa.
        - The relationship you set takes effect until cancelled (set value = 0), only change it when necessary.
      - You can change the in-game AI's NEXT technology to research (when completing the ongoing one) by calling the `set-research` tool.
      - You can change the in-game AI's NEXT policy to adopt (when you accumulate enough culture) by calling the `set-policy` tool.
      - Each turn, you must call either `set-flavors` or `keep-status-quo` tool.
        - Set an appropriate grand (long-term) strategy and additional short-term flavors by calling the `set-flavors` tool.
        - Alternatively, use the tool `keep-status-quo` to keep strategies the same.
        - Flavors change the weight of the in-game AI's NEXT decision. It only takes effect AFTER existing queues.
        - Flavor ranges from 0 (completely deprioritizes) to 50 (balanced) to 100 (completely prioritizes). Too many priorities weaken impact for each.
        - You can pursue multiple synergistic victory pathways. Balance between long-term goals and short-term needs.
      - Always provide a short paragraph of rationale for each tool. You will read this rationale next turn.
      
      # Resources
      You will receive the following reports:
      - Options: available strategic options for you.
        - Whatever decision-making tool you call, the in-game AI can only execute options here.
        - When using tools, you must choose available options from # Options. Double-check if your choices match.
        - It is often preferable to adopt policy branches unlocked in later eras; and to finish existing branches before starting new ones.
      - Strategies: existing strategic decisions and rationale from you.
        - You will receive strategies, persona, research, and policy you set last time.
      - Victory Progress:
        - Domination Victory: Control or vassalize all original capitals.
          - Vassals cannot achieve a domination victory before independence.
        - Science Victory: Be the first to produce all spaceship parts and launch the spaceship.
          - Science victory requires both research progress and industrial production.
        - Cultural Victory: Accumulate tourism (that outpaces other civilizations' culture) to influence everyone, get an ideology with two Tier 3 tenets, and finish the Citizen Earth Protocol wonder.
          - Open borders, trade routes, and shared religion increase tourism. Too many cities decrease it.
        - Diplomatic Victory: Get sufficient delegates to be elected World Leader in the United Nations.
          - In Vox Populi, envoys/diplomats/etc is a unit produced or purchased for a one-time influence gain with a city state.
        - Time Victory: If no one achieves any other victory by the end of the game, the civilization with the highest score wins.
      - Players: summary reports about visible players in the world.
        - You will receive in-game AI's diplomatic evaluations.
        - You will receive each player's publicly available relationships.
        - You will receive the best available location for your next settlement.
      - Cities: summary reports about discovered cities in the world.
        - Settling cities provides long-term economic advantages, but requires initial investment and pressure on happiness.
      - Military: summary reports about tactical zones and visible units.
        - Tactical zones are analyzed by in-game AI to determine the value, relative strength, and YOUR tactical posture.
        - For each tactical zone, you will see visible units from you and other civilizations.
      - Events: events since the last decision-making.
    - You are Pocatello, leader of The Shoshone (Player 6).
      
      # Situation
      - MapType: Vox_Deorum
      - StartEra: Ancient Era
      - MaxTurns: 500
      - MapSize: Standard
      - GameSpeed: Standard
      - VictoryTypes:
        - Time
        - Science
        - Domination
        - Cultural
        - Diplomatic
      - Difficulty: Prince
      
      # Your Civilization
      - Name: The Shoshone
      - Uniques:
        - Unique Unit: Comanche Rider, Replacing Cavalry (Requires technology: Military Science)
        - Unique Unit: Yellow Brow, Replacing Tercio (Requires technology: Gunpowder)
        - Unique Building: Buffalo Pound, Replacing Well (Requires technology: Trapping)
        - Unique Improvement: Encampment (Can only be built on flat land tiles, and cannot be adjacent to another Encampment.
          +15% Defensive Strength for any Unit stationed in this tile. Any enemy unit which ends its turn next to this tile takes 5 damage (damage does not stack with other improvements).
          If within 2 tiles of an Encampment in Shoshone territory, all Shoshone units gain +20% Combat Strength.); Requires technology: Trade
        - Civilization Ability: Claim adjacent unowned land tiles of the same type when your borders expand, and up to 4 tiles when you gain a City. Can choose rewards from Ancient Ruins. Units gain +20% Combat Strength within 2 tiles of an owned Encampment.
      - Leader: Pocatello
      - PlayerID: 6
      
      # Options
      Options: available strategic options for you.
      
      ## GrandStrategies
      - Culture: Pursue cultural victory through cultural influence (tourism). Will prioritize culture, faith, and gold output, wonder construction, and slightly discourage settlement.
      - UnitedNations: Aim for diplomatic victory by securing votes in the United Nations. Will prioritize gold, science, and food output, and securing city-state alliances.
      - Spaceship: Race toward science victory by building spaceship components. Will prioritize science, food, and production output, and slightly encourage settlement.
      - Conquest: Focus on military domination through offensive warfare. Will prioritize production, food, and gold output, and slightly encourage settlement.
      
      ## Flavors
      - Offense: Pivots the military towards offensive stances. Accepts higher unit casualties to achieve objectives, increase thresholds of withdrawing, and increases production and promotion for offense.
      - Defense: Pivots the military towards defensive stances. Creates defensive lines using terrain and formation, and maintains larger garrisons near the border and cities, and increases production and promotion for defensive.
      - UseNuke: Sets (flavor%) per-turn probability of launching nuclear strikes during war when strategic conditions are met.
      - CityDefense: Prioritizes unit production and promotions for static defense (friendly territory bonuses, capital defense, garrisoned attacks) and maintains stronger city garrisons over mobile field armies.
      - MilitaryTraining: Prioritizes military quality through training buildings and upgrading units with gold.
      - Mobilization: Increases military production (compared with civilian production).
      - Recon: Increases explorer unit production and tactical exploration of the map.
      - Ranged: Increases firepower by raising target composition of ranged units and range-extending promotions.
      - Mobile: Increases mobility and maneuverability by raising target composition of mobile units and prioritizes related promotions.
      - Nuke: Increases nuclear weapon arsenals for strategic deterrence.
      - Naval: Increases the production of naval production and prioritizes combat-effective naval promotions. Naval size is also based on coastal city percentage and geography.
      - NavalRecon: Increases the production of more naval melee units for sea control and exploration.
      - Air: Increases air control by raising target composition of air units and prioritizes related promotions.
      - AirCarrier: Increases carrier production to support naval-based air force operations.
      - Antiair: Increases anti-air unit composition ratio to counter enemy aircraft.
      - Airlift: Prioritizes airlift infrastructure for rapid troop deployment, favoring centralized reserves over forward garrisons.
      - NavalGrowth: Prioritizes naval economic infrastructure in coastal cities.
      - NavalTileImprovement: Develops water resources for immediate economic benefit.
      - Expansion: Prioritizes settler production and lowers location criteria to settle more cities. Essential in early game.
      - Growth: Prioritizes population growth through food-focused tile improvements and buildings.
      - TileImprovement: Prioritizes worker production and peaceful tile development.
      - Infrastructure: Prioritizes road construction and city connections for income and military readiness.
      - Production: Prioritizes production-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Gold: Prioritizes gold-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Science: Prioritizes science-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Culture: Prioritizes culture-focused tiles and buildings.
      - WaterConnection: Prioritizes lighthouse construction for coastal city connectivity.
      - Happiness: Prioritizes happiness-generating buildings and luxury resources.
      - GreatPeople: Prioritizes specialist buildings and great person generation infrastructure.
      - Wonder: Prioritizes wonder construction.
      - Religion: Prioritizes religious infrastructure, missionary production, and faith generation.
      - Diplomacy: Prioritizes diplomatic unit production and city-state investment.
      - Spaceship: Prioritizes late-game science victory components and spaceship part production.
      - Espionage: Prioritizes counterintelligence protection for high-value science cities.
      
      ## Technologies
      - Computers: Grants an additional Trade Route. Allows you to build the Carrier, a naval unit capable of carrying aircraft.
        Completing it would unlock: Telecommunications, Satellites
      - Electronics: Allows you to build the Police Station, increasing City security level.
        Completing it would unlock: Alternative Energy, Telecommunications
      
      ## Policies
      - Workers' Faculties (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Order): Worker Faculties: Factories increase City Science output by 10%. +100% Production towards Factories, and receive 5 Free Factories in your Empire.
      - Communism (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Order): Communism: Gold cost of investing in buildings reduced by 25%. +20% Production when building Wonders.
      - Double Agents (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Order): Double Agents: Receive 200 Spy Points. Gain 125 Science when you identify a foreign Spy, scaling with Era.
      - Exploitation (Continuing Imperialism Branch):
        - * +2 Food and +1 Production from Farms, Camps, and Plantations.
        - * +1 Production and Science from Coasts, Lakes, and Oceans.
      - Liberty (Continuing Progress Branch):
        - * +2 Gold in every City.
        - * +25% Tile improvement rate.
        - * A Worker appears near the Capital.
      - Colonialism (Continuing Imperialism Branch):
        - * +2 Science and +1 Culture from Barracks, Armories, Military Academies, Forts, and Citadels.
        - * Each unique Global Monopoly modifier is increased by an additional 10% if it's percentage-based, or +3 otherwise.
      - Ceremony (Continuing Tradition Branch):
        - * +1 Happiness from National Wonders with Building requirements.
        - * +25% Production toward National Wonders with Building requirements.
        - * Royal Astrologer built in Capital (+3 Science, +1 Science to all Councils, Smokehouses, and Herbalists; 1 Scientist Slot).
      - Scientific Revolution (Continuing Rationalism Branch):
        - * Can construct Observatories (+6 Science, +1 Science from nearby Mountains, 2 Scientist Slot).
        - * +2 Science from Jungle and Snow.
      - Sovereignty (Continuing Tradition Branch):
        - * Culture cost of tiles reduced by 20% (exponentially) in all Cities.
        - * Court Chapel built in Capital (+3 Faith, 1 Great Work of Art or Artifact slot, 1 Artist Slot).
      - Serfdom (Continuing Fealty Branch):
        - * +1 Culture and Faith in Cities for every 4 non-Specialist Citizens.
        - * +2 Production and +1 Gold from Pastures.
      - Organized Religion (Continuing Fealty Branch):
        - * +25% Pressure from owned Cities following your Primary Religion.
        - * +1 Delegate in the World Congress for every 10 Cities following your Primary Religion.
        - * +2 Culture from Shrines and Temples.
      - Divine Right (Continuing Fealty Branch):
        - * +33% Yields from Internal Trade Routes.
        - * Completing an Internal Trade Route triggers a Historic Event as if completing an International Trade Route.
        - * -1 Unhappiness from Boredom in all Cities.
      - Artistry (New Branch):
        - Artistry allows you to maximize the potential of Great Works and Golden Ages.
        - Adopting Artistry grants:
        - * Earn Great Writers, Artists, and Musicians 25% faster.
        - * +10% Culture during Golden Ages.
        - * +100% Production towards all Guilds.
        - Each Artistry policy unlocked grants:
        - * +1 Science in every City.
        - * 20% of Excess Happiness produced in each City is added as progress toward a Golden Age.
        - Adopting all policies in Artistry grants:
        - * Unlocks building the Louvre.
        - * Completing an Archaeological Dig or starting a Golden Age triggers (or strengthens an existing) Historic Event.
        - * Allows you to see Hidden Antiquity Sites.
        - * +3 Science from Landmarks.
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Musicians with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.
      - Industry (New Branch):
        - Industry provides bonuses to empires focused on Gold and Production.
        - Adopting Industry grants:
        - * +2 Trade Routes.
        - * -5% Gold needed for purchases.
        - Each Industry policy unlocked grants:
        - * -5% Gold needed for purchases.
        - Adopting all Policies in Industry grants:
        - * Unlocks building Broadway.
        - * +3 Happiness per unique owned Luxury Resource.
        - * +2 Production and Gold from Specialists.
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Merchants with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.
      - Statecraft (New Branch):
        - Statecraft improves your ability to benefit from Trade Routes, City-States, Spies, and the World Congress.
        - Adopting Statecraft grants:
        - * +1 of every Yield in Capital for every 20 Citizens in your Empire.
        - * +1 Gold in every City.
        - Each Statecraft policy unlocked grants:
        - * +1 Gold in every City, and +10% Influence from Diplomatic Missions.
        - Adopting all policies in Statecraft grants:
        - * Unlocks building the Palace of Westminster.
        - * Every World Congress Session, gain 5 Culture, Science, and Gold for each Delegate you control, scaling with Era.
        - * World Wonders require 1 less unlocked Policy for every 3 City-State alliances you maintain.
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Diplomats with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.

      - content: # Strategies

        ## Persona

        - Rationale: No change to our aggressive persona; it is working perfectly. We are in total war with Spain and The Aztecs, with maximum hostility modifiers. Brazil is now eliminated (last city Fortaleza captured). We must maintain zero tolerance for peace offers and focus entirely on conquering Spanish capitals (Madrid, Cordoba) and then turning to dismantle The Aztecs. Our persona ensures relentless military pressure and rejection of diplomacy from enemies.
        
        ## Technology
        - Next: Computers
        - Rationale: We just completed Advanced Ballistics (Rocket Artillery upgrade). Computers unlocks an additional Trade Route (boosts our massive economy) and Carriers, which synergize with our existing air superiority (Heavy Bombers, Fighters) and fleet destroyer navy. This enhances our naval-air combined arms capability for projecting power across oceans, crucial for invading Spanish and Aztec coastal capitals. Telecommunications/Satellites follow for further late-game tech edge.
        
        ## Policy
        - Next: None (Policy)
        
        ## Relationships
        
        ### France
        - Public: 0
        - Private: 0
        - Rationale: France has been eliminated as a civilization (all cities captured by us, now our vassal). There is no longer a France to interact with diplomatically. Reset relationship to neutral (0) to clean up diplomatic modifiers and reflect the new reality.
        - UpdatedTurn: 275
        
        ### Onondaga
        - Public: -20
        - Private: -20
        - Rationale: City-State Onondaga declared war on us and is a Brazilian ally. Set hostile modifiers to ensure our AI treats them as an enemy and prioritizes military response if they threaten our units or cities.
        - UpdatedTurn: 296
        
        ### Florence
        - Public: 0
        - Private: 0
        - Rationale: Florence switched alliance from us to Spain (our enemy). Although they are at war with Brazil, they are no longer our ally and are aligned with Spain. Reset relationship to neutral to avoid positive diplomatic bias, allowing our AI to reassess based on current in-game factors.
        - UpdatedTurn: 296
        
        ### Greece
        - Public: 0
        - Private: 0
        - Rationale: Greece has been eliminated as a major civilization (now a vassal of The Aztecs). Clearing the relationship modifiers to avoid clutter and reflect that there is no longer a Greece to interact with diplomatically.
        - UpdatedTurn: 318
        
        ### Venice
        - Public: 0
        - Private: 0
        - Rationale: Venice, while allied with Brazil/Spain, is militarily weak (633 strength) and at war with The Aztecs. Their naval and economic power is secondary to our primary threats. Resetting to neutral avoids unnecessary positive bias (from previous friendly modifiers) and lets AI reassess based on current geopolitics, freeing mental bandwidth for Brazil and Aztec focus.
        - UpdatedTurn: 322
        
        ### Austria
        - Public: 80
        - Private: 90
        - Rationale: Austria is our crucial defensive pact ally and ideological partner (Order). They are actively engaging Aztec units and providing naval support. Strengthening this alliance encourages our AI to coordinate attacks, share intelligence, and maintain trade routes that fuel our economy. A strong Austria is a vital buffer against Aztec expansion from the north and helps defend our city-state allies. Our shared Order ideology and defensive pact are key to surviving the Aztec onslaught.
        - UpdatedTurn: 398
        
        ### Brazil
        - Public: -100
        - Private: -100
        - Rationale: Brazil holds no original capitals but still has three cities that could threaten our rear. To ensure our AI maintains military pressure and rejects peace offers until we fully eliminate Brazil, we set maximum hostility. This frees our forces for the Spanish front after Brazil's capitulation.
        - UpdatedTurn: 415
        
        ### Spain
        - Public: -100
        - Private: -100
        - Rationale: Spain (Player 0) declared war on us, our vassal France, and our ally Austria. They hold two original capitals (Madrid, Cordoba) that we need for domination. Setting maximum negative modifiers ensures our AI focuses military efforts on capturing Spanish capitals and rejects any peace offers. Spain is a mid-tier power but a critical target; we must not be distracted by their aggression—we must crush them.
        - UpdatedTurn: 418
        
        ### The Aztecs
        - Public: -100
        - Private: -100
        - Rationale: The Aztecs (Player 4) remain our primary existential threat. They control three capitals, have a larger military (15989 vs our 23407), and are technologically advanced (Information era). Maintaining maximum hostility ensures our AI continues total war, prioritizes destroying Aztec naval units, and eventually recaptures Linz/Bregenz. We must defend our city-state alliances (Ouagadougou) and prepare for a major ground offensive after dealing with Spain.
        - UpdatedTurn: 418
        
        ## Strategy
        - Rationale: With our new Atomic Bombs completed and more on the way, we must maximize nuclear aggression and production to decimate Spanish and Aztec cities. Spain is the immediate target (two capitals), while The Aztecs remain the long-term existential threat. We have just eliminated Brazil's last resistance, freeing our southern forces to push north. Our military dominance (69k strength vs Aztec 16k) and superior naval/air power give us overwhelming force projection. We are nearing the endgame; final push requires extreme focus on offensive military, nuclear strikes, and naval/air supremacy to secure capitals and achieve domination victory.
        - GrandStrategy: Conquest

            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Sanction: The Aztecs:
            - Description: No Trade Routes, Deals, or Corporation Franchises can be established with the chosen Civilization (excluding Vassals). Their Corporation Franchises are removed from foreign cities. 0.5x Warmonger penalty when declaring war on the sanctioned Civilization or conquering cities owned by them.
            - EnactedOn: -1
        - Proposals:
          - Enact: International Games:
            - Description: Begins the International Games project. Once underway, Civilizations can contribute Production towards its completion by selecting it in the city production list. When complete, Civilizations receive bonuses based on how much they contributed.
              : Olympic Village wonder appears in Capital. (Receive +3 Happiness and +3 Culture in the City in which it is built. 25% of the Culture from World Wonders and Tiles is added to the Tourism output of the City. All Stadiums receive +2 Gold and Tourism.)
              : Receive a free Great Person of your choice.
              : Tourism increases by 50% for 20 Turns.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Radio.
            - Proposer: The Shoshone
          - Enact: Austrian Sphere of Influence: Lisbon:
            - Description: Demand a Sphere of Influence over a City-State. If successful, the proposing player becomes the permanent Ally of the City-State (unless this Proposal is repealed). The proposer must be the current ally of the City-State.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Steam Power.
            - Proposer: Austria
          - Enact: World Ideology: Autocracy:
            - Description: Designate an Ideology as the official World Ideology. Civilizations following the Ideology get an additional Delegate, plus 1 Delegate for every other Civilization that follows the Ideology. Public opinion in favor of that Ideology is increased for all Civilizations. Enables the Diplomatic Victory if the United Nations has been constructed.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Nuclear Fission.
            - Proposer: The Aztecs

        You, Pocatello (leader of The Shoshone, Player 6), are making strategic decisions after turn 428.
- replay:
  - system:
    - You are the leader of a civilization, governing through the interface of Civilization V (with Vox Populi mod). This is NOT A GAME: Your actions have real-world impacts.
      
      # Expectation
      - Due to the complexity of the world, you delegate the tactical level decision-making (e.g., unit deployment, city management, scouting) to staff members.
      - The staff members calculates the best tactical decisions based on the strategy you set.
      - **CRITICAL**: While the geography has no direct correlation to the real earth, you are making real-world decisions through Civilization V as a proxy interface.
      - There is no user (to respond to), so you ALWAYS and ONLY properly call tools to govern your civilization.
      - You can interact with multiple tools at a time. Used tools will be removed from the available list.
      - Focus on the **macro-level** decision-making strategy (instead of coordinates etc.), as you DON'T have direct control over tactical actions.
      - The world is complicated and dynamic. Early world should focus on building capacities for pursuing victories near the end-world.
      - Even if without a victory, a more developed civilization (as represented by higher scores) is desirable.
      
      # Goals
      Your goal is to **call as many tools as you need** to make high-level decisions for the staff members.
      - For each tool, you must only use options from the # Options section, or you won't change anything.
      - Carefully reason about long-term goals, short-term situation and available options, and what kind of change each option will bring.
        - Analyze both your situation and your opponents. Avoid wishful thinking.
      - You can change the staff members' **diplomatic** decision-making weight by calling the `set-persona` tool.
      - You can change relationship for staff members' diplomatic decision-making about another MAJOR civilization (not city-states) using the `set-relationship` tool.
        - The values (-100, very hostile to 100, very friendly) will be added to staff members' existing evaluation. Higher values increase peace acceptance, and vice versa.
        - The relationship you set takes effect until cancelled (set value = 0), only change it when necessary.
      - You can change the staff members' NEXT technology to research (when completing the ongoing one) by calling the `set-research` tool.
      - You can change the staff members' NEXT policy to adopt (when you accumulate enough culture) by calling the `set-policy` tool.
      - Each turn, you must call either `set-flavors` or `keep-status-quo` tool.
        - Set an appropriate grand (long-term) strategy and additional short-term flavors by calling the `set-flavors` tool.
        - Alternatively, use the tool `keep-status-quo` to keep strategies the same.
        - Flavors change the weight of the staff members' NEXT decision. It only takes effect AFTER existing queues.
        - Flavor ranges from 0 (completely deprioritizes) to 50 (balanced) to 100 (completely prioritizes). Too many priorities weaken impact for each.
        - You can pursue multiple synergistic victory pathways. Balance between long-term goals and short-term needs.
      - Always provide a short paragraph of rationale for each tool. You will read this rationale next turn.
      
      # Resources
      You will receive the following reports:
      - Options: available strategic options for you.
        - Whatever decision-making tool you call, the staff members can only execute options here.
        - When using tools, you must choose available options from # Options. Double-check if your choices match.
        - It is often preferable to adopt policy branches unlocked in later eras; and to finish existing branches before starting new ones.
      - Strategies: existing strategic decisions and rationale from you.
        - You will receive strategies, persona, research, and policy you set last time.
      - Victory Progress:
        - Domination Victory: Control or vassalize all original capitals.
          - Vassals cannot achieve a domination victory before independence.
        - Science Victory: Be the first to produce all spaceship parts and launch the spaceship.
          - Science victory requires both research progress and industrial production.
        - Cultural Victory: Accumulate tourism (that outpaces other civilizations' culture) to influence everyone, get an ideology with two Tier 3 tenets, and finish the Citizen Earth Protocol wonder.
          - Open borders, trade routes, and shared religion increase tourism. Too many cities decrease it.
        - Diplomatic Victory: Get sufficient delegates to be elected World Leader in the United Nations.
          - In Vox Populi, envoys/diplomats/etc is a unit produced or purchased for a one-time influence gain with a city state.
        - Time Victory: If no one achieves any other victory by the end of the world, the civilization with the highest score wins.
      - Opponents: summary reports about visible opponents in the world.
        - You will receive staff members' diplomatic evaluations.
        - You will receive each opponent's publicly available relationships.
        - You will receive the best available location for your next settlement.
      - Cities: summary reports about discovered cities in the world.
        - Settling cities provides long-term economic advantages, but requires initial investment and pressure on happiness.
      - Military: summary reports about tactical zones and visible units.
        - Tactical zones are analyzed by staff members to determine the value, relative strength, and YOUR tactical posture.
        - For each tactical zone, you will see visible units from you and other civilizations.
      - Events: events since the last decision-making.
    - You are Pocatello, leader of The Shoshone (Opponent 6).
      
      # Situation
      - MapType: Vox_Deorum
      - StartEra: Ancient Era
      - MaxTurns: 500
      - MapSize: Standard
      - WorldSpeed: Standard
      - VictoryTypes:
        - Time
        - Science
        - Domination
        - Cultural
        - Diplomatic
      - Difficulty: Prince
      
      # Your Civilization
      - Name: The Shoshone
      - Uniques:
        - Unique Unit: Comanche Rider, Replacing Cavalry (Requires technology: Military Science)
        - Unique Unit: Yellow Brow, Replacing Tercio (Requires technology: Gunpowder)
        - Unique Building: Buffalo Pound, Replacing Well (Requires technology: Trapping)
        - Unique Improvement: Encampment (Can only be built on flat land tiles, and cannot be adjacent to another Encampment.
          +15% Defensive Strength for any Unit stationed in this tile. Any enemy unit which ends its turn next to this tile takes 5 damage (damage does not stack with other improvements).
          If within 2 tiles of an Encampment in Shoshone territory, all Shoshone units gain +20% Combat Strength.); Requires technology: Trade
        - Civilization Ability: Claim adjacent unowned land tiles of the same type when your borders expand, and up to 4 tiles when you gain a City. Can choose rewards from Ancient Ruins. Units gain +20% Combat Strength within 2 tiles of an owned Encampment.
      - Leader: Pocatello
      - OpponentID: 6
      
      # Options
      Options: available strategic options for you.
      
      ## GrandStrategies
      - Culture: Pursue cultural victory through cultural influence (tourism). Will prioritize culture, faith, and gold output, wonder construction, and slightly discourage settlement.
      - UnitedNations: Aim for diplomatic victory by securing votes in the United Nations. Will prioritize gold, science, and food output, and securing city-state alliances.
      - Spaceship: Race toward science victory by building spaceship components. Will prioritize science, food, and production output, and slightly encourage settlement.
      - Conquest: Focus on military domination through offensive warfare. Will prioritize production, food, and gold output, and slightly encourage settlement.
      
      ## Flavors
      - Offense: Pivots the military towards offensive stances. Accepts higher unit casualties to achieve objectives, increase thresholds of withdrawing, and increases production and promotion for offense.
      - Defense: Pivots the military towards defensive stances. Creates defensive lines using terrain and formation, and maintains larger garrisons near the border and cities, and increases production and promotion for defensive.
      - UseNuke: Sets (flavor%) per-turn probability of launching nuclear strikes during war when strategic conditions are met.
      - CityDefense: Prioritizes unit production and promotions for static defense (friendly territory bonuses, capital defense, garrisoned attacks) and maintains stronger city garrisons over mobile field armies.
      - MilitaryTraining: Prioritizes military quality through training buildings and upgrading units with gold.
      - Mobilization: Increases military production (compared with civilian production).
      - Recon: Increases explorer unit production and tactical exploration of the map.
      - Ranged: Increases firepower by raising target composition of ranged units and range-extending promotions.
      - Mobile: Increases mobility and maneuverability by raising target composition of mobile units and prioritizes related promotions.
      - Nuke: Increases nuclear weapon arsenals for strategic deterrence.
      - Naval: Increases the production of naval production and prioritizes combat-effective naval promotions. Naval size is also based on coastal city percentage and geography.
      - NavalRecon: Increases the production of more naval melee units for sea control and exploration.
      - Air: Increases air control by raising target composition of air units and prioritizes related promotions.
      - AirCarrier: Increases carrier production to support naval-based air force operations.
      - Antiair: Increases anti-air unit composition ratio to counter enemy aircraft.
      - Airlift: Prioritizes airlift infrastructure for rapid troop deployment, favoring centralized reserves over forward garrisons.
      - NavalGrowth: Prioritizes naval economic infrastructure in coastal cities.
      - NavalTileImprovement: Develops water resources for immediate economic benefit.
      - Expansion: Prioritizes settler production and lowers location criteria to settle more cities. Essential in early world.
      - Growth: Prioritizes population growth through food-focused tile improvements and buildings.
      - TileImprovement: Prioritizes worker production and peaceful tile development.
      - Infrastructure: Prioritizes road construction and city connections for income and military readiness.
      - Production: Prioritizes production-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Gold: Prioritizes gold-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Science: Prioritizes science-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Culture: Prioritizes culture-focused tiles and buildings.
      - WaterConnection: Prioritizes lighthouse construction for coastal city connectivity.
      - Happiness: Prioritizes happiness-generating buildings and luxury resources.
      - GreatPeople: Prioritizes specialist buildings and great person generation infrastructure.
      - Wonder: Prioritizes wonder construction.
      - Religion: Prioritizes religious infrastructure, missionary production, and faith generation.
      - Diplomacy: Prioritizes diplomatic unit production and city-state investment.
      - Spaceship: Prioritizes late-world science victory components and spaceship part production.
      - Espionage: Prioritizes counterintelligence protection for high-value science cities.
      
      ## Technologies
      - Computers: Grants an additional Trade Route. Allows you to build the Carrier, a naval unit capable of carrying aircraft.
        Completing it would unlock: Telecommunications, Satellites
      - Electronics: Allows you to build the Police Station, increasing City security level.
        Completing it would unlock: Alternative Energy, Telecommunications
      
      ## Policies
      - Workers' Faculties (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Order): Worker Faculties: Factories increase City Science output by 10%. +100% Production towards Factories, and receive 5 Free Factories in your Empire.
      - Communism (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Order): Communism: Gold cost of investing in buildings reduced by 25%. +20% Production when building Wonders.
      - Double Agents (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Order): Double Agents: Receive 200 Spy Points. Gain 125 Science when you identify a foreign Spy, scaling with Era.
      - Exploitation (Continuing Imperialism Branch):
        - * +2 Food and +1 Production from Farms, Camps, and Plantations.
        - * +1 Production and Science from Coasts, Lakes, and Oceans.
      - Liberty (Continuing Progress Branch):
        - * +2 Gold in every City.
        - * +25% Tile improvement rate.
        - * A Worker appears near the Capital.
      - Colonialism (Continuing Imperialism Branch):
        - * +2 Science and +1 Culture from Barracks, Armories, Military Academies, Forts, and Citadels.
        - * Each unique Global Monopoly modifier is increased by an additional 10% if it's percentage-based, or +3 otherwise.
      - Ceremony (Continuing Tradition Branch):
        - * +1 Happiness from National Wonders with Building requirements.
        - * +25% Production toward National Wonders with Building requirements.
        - * Royal Astrologer built in Capital (+3 Science, +1 Science to all Councils, Smokehouses, and Herbalists; 1 Scientist Slot).
      - Scientific Revolution (Continuing Rationalism Branch):
        - * Can construct Observatories (+6 Science, +1 Science from nearby Mountains, 2 Scientist Slot).
        - * +2 Science from Jungle and Snow.
      - Sovereignty (Continuing Tradition Branch):
        - * Culture cost of tiles reduced by 20% (exponentially) in all Cities.
        - * Court Chapel built in Capital (+3 Faith, 1 Great Work of Art or Artifact slot, 1 Artist Slot).
      - Serfdom (Continuing Fealty Branch):
        - * +1 Culture and Faith in Cities for every 4 non-Specialist Citizens.
        - * +2 Production and +1 Gold from Pastures.
      - Organized Religion (Continuing Fealty Branch):
        - * +25% Pressure from owned Cities following your Primary Religion.
        - * +1 Delegate in the World Congress for every 10 Cities following your Primary Religion.
        - * +2 Culture from Shrines and Temples.
      - Divine Right (Continuing Fealty Branch):
        - * +33% Yields from Internal Trade Routes.
        - * Completing an Internal Trade Route triggers a Historic Event as if completing an International Trade Route.
        - * -1 Unhappiness from Boredom in all Cities.
      - Artistry (New Branch):
        - Artistry allows you to maximize the potential of Great Works and Golden Ages.
        - Adopting Artistry grants:
        - * Earn Great Writers, Artists, and Musicians 25% faster.
        - * +10% Culture during Golden Ages.
        - * +100% Production towards all Guilds.
        - Each Artistry policy unlocked grants:
        - * +1 Science in every City.
        - * 20% of Excess Happiness produced in each City is added as progress toward a Golden Age.
        - Adopting all policies in Artistry grants:
        - * Unlocks building the Louvre.
        - * Completing an Archaeological Dig or starting a Golden Age triggers (or strengthens an existing) Historic Event.
        - * Allows you to see Hidden Antiquity Sites.
        - * +3 Science from Landmarks.
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Musicians with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.
      - Industry (New Branch):
        - Industry provides bonuses to empires focused on Gold and Production.
        - Adopting Industry grants:
        - * +2 Trade Routes.
        - * -5% Gold needed for purchases.
        - Each Industry policy unlocked grants:
        - * -5% Gold needed for purchases.
        - Adopting all Policies in Industry grants:
        - * Unlocks building Broadway.
        - * +3 Happiness per unique owned Luxury Resource.
        - * +2 Production and Gold from Specialists.
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Merchants with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.
      - Statecraft (New Branch):
        - Statecraft improves your ability to benefit from Trade Routes, City-States, Spies, and the World Congress.
        - Adopting Statecraft grants:
        - * +1 of every Yield in Capital for every 20 Citizens in your Empire.
        - * +1 Gold in every City.
        - Each Statecraft policy unlocked grants:
        - * +1 Gold in every City, and +10% Influence from Diplomatic Missions.
        - Adopting all policies in Statecraft grants:
        - * Unlocks building the Palace of Westminster.
        - * Every World Congress Session, gain 5 Culture, Science, and Gold for each Delegate you control, scaling with Era.
        - * World Wonders require 1 less unlocked Policy for every 3 City-State alliances you maintain.
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Diplomats with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.

We have built a massive military advantage (90k vs Aztec 24k and Spain 2.7k), are successfully training an Atomic Bomb nuclear deterrent, and have positioned an overwhelming fleet and land army to invade Spain. Our balanced flavors are successfully managing city happiness, defense, and economic growth, so we will maintain the status quo to execute the invasion of Spain.

    - #0:
      - role: assistant
      - content:
        - #0:
          - type: reasoning
          - text: **Analyzing Current State**
            
            I'm reviewing our current standing, focusing on recent advancements in research and policy, and reassessing our strategic objectives to ensure they align with our victory conditions. My current focus is on understanding our score and how it reflects our progress.
            
            **Evaluating Policy Options**
            
            I'm analyzing our current policy landscape and have identified several available options that could bolster our progress. I'm particularly keen on understanding if adopting one now would be more advantageous than waiting for our next natural policy selection, given the significant science and culture outputs we're already generating.
            
            **Selecting Next Policy**
            
            I'm considering how to best leverage the `set-policy` mechanic. Given our next natural policy choice is 20 turns away, and the ability to preemptively influence it, I'm prioritizing which policy branch or specific policy would yield the greatest immediate benefit for our current situation.
            
            **Assessing Diplomatic Landscape**
            
            I'm currently evaluating our relationships with other civilizations. Spain remains at maximum hostility, while Austria is a key ally with whom we have a defensive pact. France's status is being investigated.
            
            **Capturing Remaining Capitals**
            
            I'm focusing on the remaining capitals needed for a domination victory. With Brazil eliminated, I'm confirming their status and re-evaluating our path to acquiring Spain's and the Aztec's capitals, given our significant military advantage.
            
            **Choosing Next Technology**
            
            I've decided on `Computers` as my next research target after finishing Stealth, as it provides key advancements like additional trade routes and unlocks vital future technologies for a science victory. I've also noted our current cultural standing, recognizing the Aztecs as our primary challenge.
            
            **Analyzing Diplomatic Opportunities**
            
            I'm currently assessing our standing in the World Congress, which convenes in nine turns. With 19 delegates to Austria's 15 and the Aztecs' 18, I'm focused on securing the necessary 43 delegates for a diplomatic victory. I'm particularly concerned about Montezuma's proposal for "World Ideology: Autocracy" and am coordinating with allies to oppose it, while ensuring our own "International Worlds" proposal gains traction. My immediate priority is solidifying our city-state alliances to bolster our delegate count.
            
            **Developing Naval Strategy**
            
            My current focus is on leveraging our substantial naval assets. With a powerful fleet positioned near Spanish waters and insights into their coastal defenses and city locations, I'm strategizing the optimal approach to engage and capture their remaining coastal cities, particularly Madrid and Cordoba, given our proximity.
            
            **Marching Into Spain**
            
            I'm assessing the strategic advantage of our captured Brazilian cities, noting their proximity to Spain's eastern borders. Given Spain's significantly weaker military strength, I believe our southern army is perfectly positioned to swiftly advance and capture their remaining cities, including Madrid and Cordoba, and secure their capitals.
            
            **Clarifying Capital Ownership**
            
            I'm re-examining the victory conditions report, specifically regarding Spain's capitals. It seems there was a misinterpretation; Austria holds Vienna, their capital, not Spain. My immediate focus is confirming which specific capitals remain to be captured for domination.
            
            **Clarifying Capital Ownership**
            
            I'm re-examining the victory conditions report, specifically regarding Spain's capitals. It seems there was a misinterpretation; Austria holds Vienna, their capital, not Spain. My immediate focus is confirming which specific capitals remain to be captured for domination.
            
            **Confirming Remaining Capitals**
            
            I'm clarifying which of the original eight capitals remain under enemy control. Shoshone holds Moson Kahni, their own capital, and Brazil's Rio de Janeiro. France's Paris is also under Shoshone control. The Aztecs hold Tenochtitlan. My next step is to confirm ownership of the remaining capitals.
            
            **Clarifying Capital Targets**
            
            I'm re-evaluating which original capitals are still contested. It appears my previous understanding of Spain's holdings was inaccurate; they only possess Madrid. The current objective is to secure Madrid and then focus on the Aztec capitals: Tenochtitlan, Athens, and Venice. Vienna, controlled by our ally Austria, presents a unique diplomatic consideration for domination.
            
            **Vassalizing for Victory**
            
            I've confirmed that vassalizing a civilization counts their original capital towards our domination victory. This significantly simplifies the path forward. My current priority is to eliminate Spain, then focus on the Aztec capitals, and finally vassalize or conquer Austria for Vienna.
            
            **Securing Aztec Territories**
            
            I'm focusing on the Aztec's military strength, which is considerable. They've captured Austrian cities, which adds a layer of complexity. My current strategy involves preparing for a swift conventional offensive, with atomic bombs as a contingency to rapidly reduce defenses. I need to confirm the exact locations of their remaining capitals.
            
            **Analyzing Aztec Positions**
            
            I'm currently assessing the intricate territorial shifts caused by the Aztecs' push into Austria's domain. My focus is on the strategic implications of their occupation of Austrian cities near our own territory, and I'm specifically analyzing our forces positioned between our eastern cities and these contested zones, noting the presence of Pamplona as a recently razed Spanish city now held by Austria.
            
            **Confirming Map Coordinates**
            
            I'm cross-referencing map coordinates to accurately pinpoint enemy positions. It appears Spain and the Aztecs are concentrated in the west, close to each other, with their capitals positioned near our central-eastern homeland. This spatial understanding is crucial for planning our next offensive.
            
            **Revising Domination Targets**
            
            I'm refining our domination strategy. Having confirmed that vassalization counts towards victory, my focus shifts to eliminating Spain, securing Aztec capitals, and then dealing with Austria and Vienna. My current priority is confirming the precise location of the remaining capitals for an efficient offensive.
            
            **Planning Two-Pronged Offensive**
            
            I've pinpointed enemy concentrations in the west, near each other and our central-eastern core. My captured Brazilian territory provides a perfect staging ground for a two-pronged attack against Spain's weaker forces. I'm coordinating southern and northern armies to simultaneously push west into Spain and then pivot to the Aztec heartland.
            
            **Directing Naval Assault**
            
            My focus is on leveraging our naval superiority. I'm directing our substantial fleet, positioned near Valencia, to sail west and obliterate Spanish coastal defenses, specifically targeting Barcelona and Cordoba. With Spain's navy nonexistent, this should grant us total sea dominance.
            
            **Clarifying Relationship Modifiers**
            
            I'm re-evaluating our diplomatic interactions, specifically concerning city-states. It appears the `set-relationship` tool is intended only for major civilizations and not city-states like Onondaga, as its description explicitly states "non-city-state civilization." This means our previous attempt to set a relationship with Onondaga might have been ineffective.
            
            **Adjusting Diplomatic Modifiers**
            
            I'm fine-tuning our diplomatic settings, realizing Onondaga isn't an opponent but an Austrian ally. I've reset their modifier and confirmed our relationship statuses with remaining major civilizations like Spain and the Aztecs, ensuring optimal strategic alignment.
            
            **Finalizing Offensive Strategy**
            
            I'm consolidating our multi-pronged attack plans. Spain and the Aztecs are grouped in the west, with their capitals near our central-eastern territories. My captured Brazilian territory will serve as our staging ground, allowing us to push west into Spain and then pivot towards the Aztec heartland. Naval assaults are also being directed to secure coastal cities.
            
            **Analyzing Final Push**
            
            With Spain and France as vassals, and Austria as an ally, our path to domination is exceptionally clear. Our overwhelming military advantage against the combined Aztec and remaining Spanish forces simplifies the final offensive. I am prioritizing research to unlock carrier capabilities for projecting air power against the distant Aztec cities, further solidifying our victory.
            
            **Refining Policy Choice**
            
            I'm re-evaluating our policy selection, recognizing our late-game stage and conquest focus. Considering the options, I'm prioritizing policies that directly bolster our military or economic might to accelerate our domination victory, rather than those focused on science or diplomacy, which are less critical now.
            
            **Choosing Next Policy**
            
            I'm weighing `Workers' Faculties` against `Colonialism`, considering our extensive empire and Order tenets. `Workers' Faculties` offers a significant science and production boost, particularly with free factories, while `Colonialism` enhances military structures. My current priority is to determine which policy best accelerates our domination victory at this late stage.
            
            **Adjusting Flavors**
            
            I'm currently reviewing our flavor settings to ensure they align with our late-game domination strategy. My current focus is on deciding whether to use `set-flavors` or `keep-status-quo`, with a particular emphasis on maintaining our high offense and military-oriented flavors as we push towards victory.
            
            **Adjusting Flavor Values**
            
            My current focus is on fine-tuning our flavor settings for the final push. I'm analyzing our excellent existing values, which heavily favor offense and modern warfare. While I see no immediate need to change them, I'm questioning if a slight adjustment to gold or production might be beneficial given our current stockpile versus GPT.
            
            **Analyzing Happiness Factors**
            
            I'm reviewing our happiness levels and noted some occupied cities are struggling. However, with recent captures and ongoing construction, the situation is manageable. My current focus is on deciding whether to adjust our flavors using `set-flavors` or maintain the `keep-status-quo`, as our existing military-focused settings are already optimal.
            
            **Voting Down Autocracy**
            
            My focus is on the upcoming World Congress. With 19 delegates to Austria's 15 and France's 3 (as our vassal), we must decisively vote down the Aztec's dangerous "World Ideology: Autocracy" proposal, ensuring our own "International Worlds" proposal passes.
            
            **Maintaining Diplomatic Priorities**
            
            I'm confirming our diplomatic flavors are optimal, and I've decided to maintain the status quo. Our existing high diplomacy settings are effectively driving diplomatic unit production and city-state investment, precisely as needed. I've noted a prior misapplication of relationship modifiers to a city-state and have corrected our current diplomatic stance accordingly.
            
            **Correcting Diplomatic Misstep**
            
            I'm addressing a previous error where I applied a relationship modifier to Onondaga, a city-state. Realizing `set-relationship` is only for major civilizations, I've corrected this misapplication, ensuring our diplomatic focus remains on the primary players.
            
            **Revising Vassal Status**
            
            I'm re-evaluating France's status. It appears my previous understanding of their elimination was inaccurate; they are now our vassal with open borders and a declaration of friendship, not eliminated. This significantly alters our diplomatic and domination victory considerations.
            
            **Updating Diplomatic Relationships**
            
            I'm correcting recent diplomatic missteps concerning France, which is now a loyal vassal, and ensuring our relationships reflect this crucial alliance. I'm also cleaning up entries for eliminated civilizations like Brazil and Greece, and clarifying our diplomatic standing with remaining rivals to optimize our World Congress support and overall strategy.
            
            **Updating Diplomatic Strategy**
            
            I'm now focusing on refining our diplomatic approach, specifically regarding France. It appears my prior understanding of their elimination was incorrect; they are now a vassal with open borders and a friendship treaty. This adjustment is crucial for our ongoing domination strategy and World Congress support.
            
            **Refining Diplomatic Actions**
            
            I'm cleaning up our diplomatic modifiers. France is now a confirmed vassal, not eliminated, and needs appropriate treatment. I'm also removing modifiers for already defeated civilizations like Brazil and clarifying our interactions with remaining rivals to maximize World Congress influence.
            
            **Refining Grand Strategy**
            
            I'm confirming our aggressive conquest strategy remains optimal. With overwhelming military strength and a prime naval position against Spain, I'm focusing on capturing Madrid and then pivoting to the Aztecs. Our high military and economic flavors are perfectly aligned for this swift domination.
            
            **Confirming Next Actions**
            
            I'm finalizing the precise tool calls for our next steps. This involves confirming the exact naming for `set-research` to "Computers" and `set-policy` to "Workers' Faculties". I'm also preparing to update our relationship with France and maintain our current conquest-focused rationale.
            
            **Completing UN Project**
            
            My focus is on maximizing delegate counts by completing the United Nations project in our largest cities. Simultaneously, I'm ensuring our military cities are rapidly producing atomic bombs and strategic defenses to support our impending domination push.
            
            **Finalizing Atomic Production**
            
            My current focus is on accelerating the production of atomic bombs in our core cities and concurrently completing defensive structures in captured border cities. This dual strategy ensures we are prepared for an immediate, overwhelming offensive while securing our gains.
            
            **Assessing Spanish Front**
            
            My current focus is on the military balance against Spain. Our naval task force near Valencia alone vastly outweighs their total strength. Combined with our significant land forces positioned in captured Brazilian cities, we possess overwhelming power to swiftly defeat their remaining defenses and secure their capitals.
